- Interview projection — `orchestrator/mind_projection.py` (263 lines). A self-spec is a first-person portrait of how the user operates; the assistant needs second-person imperative directives. `project_self_spec` (`:178`) makes one model call (`model_dispatch.invoke_chat`, breadth slot) driven by `PROJECTION_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`:73`), which transforms the portrait into whitelisted `## `-sections: What to Challenge Me On, How to Deliver Pushback, My Red Lines, Mission Alignment, Off Limits, Private Context (`ALLOWED_SECTIONS`, `:60`). `validate_directives` (`:159`) enforces the whitelist and 200–8000-char bounds (`MIN_CHARS`/`MAX_CHARS`, `:70-71`). `compose_projected_mind_md` (`:229`) assembles the final file as guided-base plus a projected block delimited by the projected marker. Backs `POST /api/mind/project`. 29 tests.

The projection's source resolution (`server.py:1383`) reads `mindspec/self-spec.md` when present, otherwise back-fills it from a raw-interview `mind.md` (one that exists, is customized, and carries neither guided nor projected marker) — archiving that content to `self-spec.md` first. On this checkout `self-spec.md` does **not exist** (status *planned*): the mindspec directory holds only `default-mindspec.md` (50 KB canonical library) and `mind-template.md`. The projection is fully coded and tested, but its live trigger requires first running `/framework mindspec-interview` to write the self-spec. The endpoint docstring claims the interview writes it "since 2026-07-01" (`server.py:1373`); that claim could not be verified from the files alone.

**Where `mind.md` sits relative to its neighbors.** `mind.md` is the user's *values*, injected conditionally and authoritatively over the default Mind Seeds in `boot/boot.md`. It is not the interview library (`mindspec/`) and not the boot system prompt. The guided wizard and the projection both write `mind.md`; the interview library is read-only reference material that feeds the projection.

**Normal operation vs. failure.** Normal path: a turn resolves a style_id, `_compose_output_style` renders the gear-appropriate block, it splices into the step prompt at the correct site; if values are enabled, `mind.md` (privacy-filtered) appends to boot content. Failure paths all degrade to empty: missing PyYAML → styles disabled, pipeline unaffected; corrupt `custom-styles.json` → empty custom store; malformed profile → `compose` raises, `_compose_output_style` returns `""`; unknown `/style` id → passes as text; missing `mind.md` or values-off → no values block. No single failure in this subsystem can break the analytical pipeline. That is the whole reliability posture, and it is qualitative — none of these degradations is instrumented with counters or success-rate metrics; the named failure mode (styles silently going dark) is caught by the guards, not measured.

### Evidence & Verification

The subsystem carries 123 unit tests across 12 files, all present and verified at this checkout:

| File | Tests | Surface |
|---|---|---|
| `test_style_assembly.py` | 9 | registry/axes/schema parse; gear branch; delta math |
| `test_style_store.py` | 8 | CRUD; atomic write; corrupt-file degradation; PATCHABLE whitelist |
| `test_style_injection.py` | 7 | the two boot injection sites + step gating |
| `test_style_resolution.py` | 7 | the effective-style-id precedence chain |
| `test_style_oneoff.py` | 6 | `/style <id>` and `/style off` parsing |
| `test_style_cards.py` | 4 | picker card labels |
| `test_style_trace.py` | 2 | trace capture |
| `test_style_audience.py` | 7 | honne/tatemae folding |
| `test_style_mindmd.py` | 3 | values-block injection + private filter |
| `test_mind_guided.py` | 31 | question registry, composer, marker parse, overwrite guard |
| `test_mind_projection.py` | 29 | prompt, whitelist validation, projected-marker preservation, private-heading pin |
| `test_mind_endpoints.py` | 10 | the six HTTP endpoints, provenance derivation |

To confirm the subsystem works, run the style and mind suites (`python3 -m unittest orchestrator.tests.test_style_assembly …`). To confirm the substance-first exclusion mechanically, read `boot.py:8711` — the step tuple is the proof that evaluator and verifier never receive a style block. To confirm degradation, corrupt `data/custom-styles.json` and confirm the picker still loads (empty store).

**Explicitly untested / unverified surfaces:**

- The situational-delta / register chain is unit-tested in isolation but has no runtime driver (`style_register` hardcoded `"written"`, `style_deltas` `None`). Its end-to-end behavior is dead in production. The 9 assembly tests exercise `resolve_demeanor` and the conversational overlay directly; nothing exercises them through a live turn, because nothing can.
- The `mind_projection` end-to-end path depends on a real `self-spec.md` that is absent on a fresh install. The 29 tests mock the model call; the live trigger (a completed MindSpec interview writing the self-spec) is not verifiable from this checkout, and the "since 2026-07-01" claim in the endpoint docstring is unconfirmed.
- All file I/O and path derivation are exercised only on macOS (M4 Max). See platform compatibility below.

### Expert Reading Notes

Read in this order. First `style_assembly.compose()` (`:201`) for the gear branch — this is the entire style-shape logic in one function. Then the two `boot.py` injection sites, `:8539` (gears 1-2, high) and `:8711` (gears 3-4, after substance, producing steps only) — this is where the substance-first ordering physically lives, and the step tuple at `:8711` is the load-bearing exclusion. Then `_resolve_effective_style_id` (`:7088`) for the precedence chain. For values, read `load_boot_md:1316-1324` and `_filter_private_values:95`.

Where future changes most likely cause drift:

- The register/delta chain. If someone wires `style_register` or `style_deltas` into the context package (`boot.py:7767-7768`), the currently-dead conversational overlay and delta math go live at once, and the demeanor a user sees at gears 1-2 will change. Any change there needs the assembly tests re-run against a live turn, not just in isolation.
- Two `WORKSPACE` derivations. `boot.py` uses `expanduser("~/ora/")` (`:110`) for `mind.md`; `style_store`/`style_assembly` use `__file__` (`:24`, `:25`). A refactor that "unifies" them could break either the worktree-isolation the `__file__` form gives tests, or the live-install targeting the `expanduser` form gives boot. Verify both before consolidating.
- The `PRIVATE_VALUES_HEADING` ↔ `ALLOWED_SECTIONS` pin. `boot.py:87` and `mind_projection.py:66` must agree on `"Private Context"`; the unit test at `test_mind_projection.py:164-165` enforces it. Rename one without the other and private directives could leak into ordinary conversations.

Known documentation drift, so you don't chase ghosts:

- `style_assembly.py`'s module docstring is stale. Lines 8-10 claim "Nothing in the pipeline imports it yet … [the wiring] sit[s] on top of this later." This is false at 7a5e8f40: `boot.py::_compose_output_style` imports and calls `compose()`. Trust the code; the wiring exists.
- `config/interface.json` does not hold the Output Styles tab. It is layout + theme only. The tab is a `settings-panel.js` / `styles-pane.js` construct. Do not repeat the interface.json premise.
- The `styles.default_id` default is `"explainer"`, not empty. Styles ship on.
- The full-word precedence line (`values floor > completeness floor > craft floor > substance/findings > style`) lives only in the module docstring (`:13`). The lines actually injected into prompts are condensed variants (`:164`, `:175`) and differ between the gear-2 and gear-3/4 blocks (`substance` vs `findings`). Quote the injected form, not the docstring form, if the exact string matters.

Treat the vault's [[Reference — Ora Output Styles Pane]] (2026-07-01) as canonical over both code comments and this chapter for the pane's UI; the `Fiction register` section it lists is a *deferred* placeholder, not built.

### Platform compatibility

All verification for this subsystem was performed on macOS (Apple M4 Max, macOS 26.3). Every non-macOS surface below is labeled from code inspection, not from an off-Mac run; where it was not actually executed elsewhere, it is marked untested.

- `mind.md` / `custom-styles.json` / `self-spec.md` path resolution (WORKSPACE derived from `__file__` in `style_assembly`/`style_store`; from `os.path.expanduser("~/ora/")` in `boot.py`; server paths via `os.path.join`). **macOS: works. Windows-native: untested. WSL: untested. Linux: untested.** All paths are built with `os.path.join` and are separator-agnostic, so they are likely portable, but only exercised on macOS. The two distinct WORKSPACE derivations (noted above) are a portability seam to check off-Mac.
- Atomic writes (tmp + `os.replace` in `style_store._save_all`, the mind endpoints, and `mind.md` writes). **macOS: works. Windows-native: untested. WSL: untested. Linux: untested.** `os.replace` is atomic within a filesystem on both POSIX and Windows, so behavior should hold, but it was not run off-Mac. No `fcntl` or symlink use in this subsystem.
- PyYAML dependency (`style_assembly.load_registry`). **macOS: works. Windows-native: untested. WSL: untested. Linux: untested.** Pure-Python, no native deps. The import is guarded (`:57` raises if absent); a missing-PyYAML environment silently *disables* Output Styles via the exception-swallowing in `_compose_output_style` and the accept-on-failure in `_is_known_style_id`, rather than crashing. Confirm PyYAML is present in any non-Mac deploy env, or the whole style layer goes dark without an error.
- `mind_projection` model call (`model_dispatch.invoke_chat`, breadth slot). **macOS: works. Windows-native: untested. WSL: untested. Linux: untested.** The projection transform is provider-agnostic (any breadth-slot model). Only the *local-model* backing inherits the MLX / Apple-Silicon constraint; a cloud breadth slot removes even that dependency. The Mac-specificity here is the local-inference layer, not the projection code.
- Server endpoints (Flask, localhost:5000 — the six `/api/mind*` and `/api/styles*` routes). **macOS: works. Windows-native: untested. WSL: untested. Linux: untested.** Standard Flask with a portable localhost binding; not specifically exercised off macOS for this subsystem.

# Part III — Knowledge, Tools & Media

## 8. Vault, RAG, Conversations & Persistent State

- **Current destructive-state preparation.** Protected mutations do not ask for approval until their exact durable target is usable.
- A Stealth Delete Forever request binds the Dialogue's logical identity, deletion state, and authoritative tag, then compares that exact approved state again while holding the lifecycle lock immediately before installing the tombstone.
- **Session-export privacy boundary.** `POST /api/session/export` refuses any request containing a private vault-root, sessions-root, raw-conversation, or Node-program HTTP field before entering Dialogue lifecycle or touching filesystem/export paths. Those dependencies remain substitutable only through the internal Python seam.

### Problem

A stateless model cannot supply continuity by itself. Ora must reconstruct the exact history a Dialogue branch may see, add explicit and discovered sources without violating privacy, fit complete evidence units inside the selected endpoint's real request limit, and deliver the same server-authoritative context to every processing path.

The hard cases are structural. A fork must see the ancestor prefixes that existed when each branch was made, never later ancestor turns. Explicit contributors must stay ordered, cutoff-safe, fairly represented, and visibly accounted for when missing, withheld, or deferred. Global retrieval must not rediscover the current Dialogue, its ancestry, a contributor, or a contributor's ancestry through another row. The stored retrieval document must preserve the full exchange even though the vector is intentionally oriented around context plus the user's prompt.

Lifecycle language must also match consequences. Standard and Private Close is reversible hiding, Exit Stealth is navigation, and Stealth Delete Forever is an Ora-managed purge that leaves surviving descendants detached. Explicit exports and copies already held by providers, Git, backups, or other external systems are outside that purge boundary.

### Design Decision & Tradeoffs

Five decisions carry the subsystem.

**The server owns effective history.** Each envelope stores local messages only. A true fork starts with `messages=[]`, local numbering at 1, the direct parent, and an immutable `fork_point_message_count` in that parent's local coordinates. Read-time recursion applies every ancestry cutoff before appending the child's local turns. Later ancestor turns cannot leak, the parent remains unchanged, and `fork_point_chunk_id` is legacy metadata only. Raw exchanges are authoritative; there is no accepted-decision store or inference pass.

**Context is packed as complete units against the endpoint, not a nominal character cap.** The Dialogue maximum is 200,000 tokens, but the safe request can be smaller after required payload, output allowance, retry headroom, images, provider constraints, and safety reserves. An unknown endpoint fails small. Recent local turns and the fork frontier lead, then promoted contributor units, older history, and global RAG. Initial 50/40/10 planning shares for history/contributors/global are soft; unused space returns to a common pool. No turn or indexed note chunk is truncated mid-unit.

**Explicit and global sources remain different lanes.** Contributors are ordered, identity-deduplicated, and uncapped. Dialogue contributors resolve recursively and cutoff-safely; atomic-note contributors use indexed hierarchical-content-provenance chunks. All references remain inventoried as available, missing, withheld, or deferred, and source-fair scheduling prevents one large contributor from crowding out the rest. Global RAG is lower priority and excludes the current Dialogue, all current ancestors, all contributors, and all contributor ancestors source-wide.

**Storage and semantic orientation are separate.** A conversation row's stored document is Context + User + Assistant. Its separately supplied vector input is bounded Context + User. The assistant is therefore returned with the retrieved evidence but does not steer the embedding. Live writes and historical replay target the same contract. Existing rows still require validated replay before this split is fully materialized across the historical collection; this chapter does not claim that production cutover has occurred.

**Aside help is an isolated public corpus, not another memory lane.** Aside may retrieve only five repository-tracked files from `help/`: `user-guide.md`, `accessible-overview.md`, `install-guide.md`, `install-manual.md`, and `install-recovery.md`. They use the logical owner `help`, and collision checks refuse a collection or alias owned by another corpus. Vault notes, Dialogue rows, and private RAG never enter this search. Retrieved excerpts exist only in the current Aside model call; they are not appended to conversation history or written back to any index. The tradeoff is intentionally limited coverage in exchange for a boundary a user can understand and audit.

Privacy belongs to each complete Inquiry/Findings pair, not the Dialogue. Ordinary history needs adjacent halves with the same valid privacy; Dialogue and local turn are reconstructed from the canonical envelope and message order, without requiring matching persisted turn or chunk fields. A Dialogue may mix Standard and Private turns, and its composer tag never fills missing or conflicting older privacy.

Access is cumulative: Standard may use Standard; Private may also use Private; Stealth may use all three. Ordinary history and contributors silently filter missing, conflicting, or too-strict pair privacy. Library/search use surface-specific pre-content predicates. Full owner authentication is reserved for fork creation, current-pair extraction and review derivatives, runtime-indexed derivatives, retagging, and current/full export.

An archived Dialogue contributes only complete, cutoff-safe pairs with matching permitted privacy; archived notes and global rows stay excluded. A note or row claiming to be an Ora-managed derivative needs its complete exact owner tuple. A partial or false claim cannot fall back to Standard or restore missing content.

### Implementation

**Protected retrieval rebuild (current Model boundary).** `retrieval_rebuild_start` prepares an immutable inactive-profile plan before approval: supported provider, non-empty model, exact positive-integer dimension, valid active retrieval configuration, and a readable regular non-symlink `re-embed-local.py`. The approval binds provider/model/dimension/profile and script identity, plus the exact vector-store, Chroma configuration, and script pre-state. `_spawn_reembed` rechecks both job availability and the complete plan under the existing rebuild mutex before claiming worker state. Immediately before subprocess launch, the protected effect reauthenticates those storage identities; the command carries explicit profile/provider/model/dimension arguments and `--activate-on-success`. No filesystem lock is held over the store for the entire rebuild.

A plan mismatch or competing job after authorization closes as failed without overwriting the competing job. Thread-start failure releases the claimed state; worker failure reports its actual outcome and receipt. Process success reports `requires_restart`, not historical-row validation or a completed production migration. These receipt and admission safeguards do not themselves roll back a partially executed rebuild.

**Envelope and effective-history resolution.** `orchestrator/conversation_memory.py` owns the envelope and effective history. Ordinary and visual writes distinguish an absent envelope from one that cannot be decoded, read, or validated as an object with a messages list. Only absence permits a new envelope. An unreadable existing file remains at its original path, unchanged; the writer reports failure instead of moving it aside or replacing it with an empty history. Error marking follows the same preservation rule. Valid writes retain the existing lock and atomic-replacement path. Startup recovery uses those same mechanisms and retains its pending source until the recovered chunk and envelope are both saved.

Effective history yields only adjacent complete Inquiry/Findings pairs with matching valid privacy, then derives owner and local turn from the canonical envelope and order for downstream actions; ordinary history does not authenticate persisted owner, turn, or chunk agreement. Orphans, malformed privacy, and disagreements stay stored for recovery but do not enter model history.

New forks store no copied parent messages; recursive reads enforce each parent cutoff. Fork creation separately validates every inherited complete pair's reconstructed Dialogue/turn owner and matching saved chunk/privacy. The child starts at local turn one while parent, project, contributors, canonical content, recovery data, and source identity stay intact. Stealth purge detaches children; it strips a legacy copied prefix only on an exact match, preserving ambiguous local content without authorizing it for a weaker lane.

**Contributor resolution and inventory.** Conversation references resolve by stable identity and recursively rebuild cutoff-safe history. An ordinary contributor pair needs matching permitted privacy; its envelope supplies owner and order, not persisted turn/chunk agreement. A Dialogue tag cannot fill a gap. Atomic notes use indexed HCP chunks; Ora-managed derivatives require their complete source-exchange tuple.

Deduplication precedes fair scheduling. The private bundle inventory records missing, withheld, and deferred status internally. Only selected provenance/source labels face the model, and ordinary trace exposes no candidates, so an unavailable contributor need not produce a visible notice.

**Context assembly.** The server packs complete units against an endpoint-safe budget, prioritizing recent local context/fork frontier, contributors, older history, then global results. Before any consumer sees content, `filter_conversation_history_for_tag` removes a pair with unknown or too-strict privacy.

Dense and lexical conversation retrieval use exact `turn_privacy` predicates plus a second row check before candidacy. Knowledge retrieval groups rows by path and admits a claimed derivative only when every row has one matching eligible owner tuple, before Chroma distance. SQLite lexical joins admitted paths or exact turn privacy before scoring.

Library and search surfaces apply their privacy/ownership rule before title, snippet, body score, or distance, with a stale-backend backstop. Global exclusions remain source-wide and ranking cannot restore an ineligible candidate. This governs calls when made; Phase 4 automatic global semantic retrieval is not active.

The interactive Library browser applies project (including explicit Commons), inclusive activity dates, privacy, lifecycle, relationship, and local-restriction filters on the server before limiting. Rows carry structured privacy, provenance, lifecycle, and relationship state; `closed` is only the authority for active/inactive, indexed archive remains distinct, and no current authority supplies a local-restriction value, so that facet is explicitly unavailable with zero counts. Parent, direct child, and sibling are distinct; contributor, incoming direct-related, and shared exact-project relationships are added only from stored references and project IDs, never from inferred Engram lineage. A process-local stat-keyed parsed-envelope snapshot serves existing Dialogue inventory callers: unchanged inventory reads parse no envelopes, one changed envelope reparses itself, empty Library browse skips body-regex cleaning, and the Library consumes the inventory's already parsed envelope/effective history rather than reopening every file.

The separate shared Library adapter reuses those live Dialogue rows, enumerates Engrams nonsemantically in one knowledge-collection read, and takes one complete in-memory enumeration of each registered project's file inventory. Engram paths share one filesystem enumeration per parent for alias canonicalization, missing-file and modified-time truth; POSIX symlink leaves collapse to their targets, while Windows retains full-leaf canonicalization. Only then does it normalize and page the combined result; the existing project-files route separately keeps its first-500 default and explicit pagination. The complete Engram universe leaves exact editability unavailable, and only returned Engram rows receive current `R_OK`/`W_OK` hydration after paging. Its relationship view opens SQLite in read-only mode without constructing the graph writer. A full YAML reconciliation records its coverage watermark before scanning and alone can set completeness true; traversal errors and direct non-full-sync graph mutations keep it incomplete until another successful full reconciliation. The read view then labels the snapshot fresh, stale, incomplete, or unavailable against that evidence and the inspected Markdown universe. Preview and editability are routing descriptors only: browsing neither reads file bodies into the response nor mutates source content.

**Conversation indexing.** `orchestrator/conversation_chunk.py` defines live/replay exchange records. A live envelope pair receives one Dialogue, local turn, chunk, and privacy owner; raw, chunk, manifest, and Chroma representations bind the corresponding available fields. Chroma stores Context + User + Assistant but embeds bounded Context + User. Its turn-privacy predicate and row check run before document or distance leaves the collection.

Document-processing output promoted into the knowledge corpus uses a stricter derivative owner: `artifact_kind: conversation_runtime_derivative`, `managed_by: ora`, stable source Dialogue, source chunk, positive local turn, and exact privacy, with the controlled Private tag when applicable. If any row for a path claims that ownership, every row for the path must authenticate the same owner and be eligible. A lone source field, conflicting tag, malformed turn, or mixed owner excludes the whole path rather than falling back to ordinary-note privacy.

Metadata remains denormalized and filterable; authoritative content remains in the envelope and raw exchange. Retagging changes only representations that still prove the exact old owner. It never rewrites canonical text, fork lineage, project association, contributor order, raw recovery identity, or a user-authored note merely because that note mentions the same source. `docs/conversation-file-format.py` is the field reference, not a claim that Pydantic validates runtime writes.

**Protected privacy repair preparation.** `python3 -m orchestrator.tools.chroma_source_rebuild protected-inactive-copy` is a separately invoked operator CLI for preparing a metadata-only repaired conversation collection. Its explicit JSON authority plan names distinct existing source and fresh inactive target physical collections and supplies complete Private and Standard row-ID arrays, each bound to its expected count and sorted-ID digest. The authority must cover the source collection exactly. Missing or unknown fields, duplicate or overlapping IDs, count/digest drift, unknown or omitted source IDs, unsafe names, a pre-existing target, conflicting source privacy metadata, invalid payload/profile/vector shape, or source/target identity drift are refused before it can be treated as success.

The current source collection is the strict survival allowlist. The command creates the target in the same Chroma store and copies its exact IDs, stored retrieval documents, stored vectors, collection metadata, and all nonprivacy row metadata; it changes only `turn_privacy`, `tag`, and `tag_private`. Exact audits cover IDs, documents, collection/nonprivacy metadata, and stored vectors, with the existing Chroma-copy tolerance of no more than one float32 ULP per vector component. If any later check fails, cleanup first rechecks the created target's identity and removes only that target. The path does not embed, replay, contact a network, read or change runtime configuration, stop Ora, backfill envelopes, promote the target, or cut over the active mapping.

For the present 40,177-row corpus, the owner has authorized 4,107 Private and 36,070 Standard rows. That decision knowingly classifies 16,534 propagation-only Private rows as Standard, preserves all 698 faithful empty-sided turns without inventing missing text, and excludes 884 recovery-only rows because they are absent from the active source allowlist. This is authority and repository capability, not a completed data operation: no live inactive copy, envelope backfill, cutover, or deployment has occurred.

**Current-turn extraction.** After a response and its envelope pair are durable, the background worker reacquires the process-local Dialogue lifecycle lock and rereads canonical local history. It must match the requested current turn number, chunk id, content, and exact non-Stealth privacy before running. The runtime pipeline receives only that one authenticated pair; prior history cannot be smuggled in as a substitute source. An authentication or extraction failure is logged without invalidating the delivered exchange.

Approved staging notes and pending review records preserve the same exact owner tuple. `server/review.py` acquires the source Dialogue's cross-process lifecycle lock for approve, edit, and reject, rereads the record, and refuses an owner change. Displayed-turn retag uses that lock too, so publication and retag serialize instead of racing a stale privacy label into the vault.

**Aside help retrieval.** `orchestrator/help_retrieval.py` owns the exact five-file whitelist, deterministic heading-aware chunks, indexing, and lexical fallback. Chunks are bounded at 1,600 characters; returned excerpts are bounded at 760 characters each and the assembled help context at 3,200. Startup refreshes the help-owned Chroma rows only when the embedding pipeline is ready. It upserts changed deterministic chunks, deletes stale help-owned rows only after a successful pass, and aborts rather than crossing an ownership collision. If Chroma or embeddings are unavailable, lexical ranking reads the same five files directly. `server.app` adds the selected excerpts to the `/api/scratchpad` system context for that request only. Help failure is logged and the ordinary Aside answer continues without excerpts.

Aside requires the selected Dialogue's `conversation_id` and keeps at most five complete query/answer pairs in that Dialogue's existing `SidebarWindow`. The per-window transaction covers help lookup, history read, model call, and append so overlapping requests preserve causal order. The existing Dialogue lifecycle lock spans the transaction; a deleted Dialogue returns `410`, and Delete Forever cannot clear its window and then receive a late append. Status and clear address the same window through `panel_id` equal to that Dialogue ID and use the same lifecycle coordination. Purge clears that exact in-memory window; shutdown clears all windows. The separate legacy pipeline sidebar continues to use its existing panel keys. Aside accepts only prompt and Dialogue identity and carries no Run or transfer fields. Successful responses echo the Dialogue ID and report `persisted: false` and `authoritative: false` inside the surface contract. This model context is separate from the browser's ten-pair display described in Chapter 2; neither is durable Dialogue history.

**Supplemental handling.** A supplemental request never runs a fresh query or appends a result beyond the budget. It promotes unseen, already-validated deferred contributor/global whole units and repacks the same call boundary. It stops on a repeated gap, no new eligible unit, no whole-unit fit, or resolution; there is no numeric request cap. The first three conditions yield a local `COVERAGE GAP`.

**Lifecycle and navigation.** Standard/Private Close sets `closed: true`; retained Dialogues disappear from the ordinary sidebar and remain restorable in Manage. The sidebar shows the current project's non-Stealth Dialogues plus only the active Stealth Dialogue. Exit Stealth selects the latest readable direct parent, even if that parent is Stealth, or creates a fresh Standard Dialogue; it does not close or delete. Delete Forever invokes protected Stealth purge even with descendants, which detach and retain only local turns. Ora-managed state is the purge boundary; deliberate exports and external/provider/Git/backup copies remain.

Paid Replicate video and style work belongs to this same live Dialogue lifecycle. Delete Forever treats the shared queue as a synchronous pre-purge barrier.
Every bound prediction must already be terminal or receive provider-cancelled or terminal confirmation before any purge or success response.
On failure Ora retains `jobs.json` and the exact provider/Dialogue/job binding, unwinds the temporary server tombstone for retry, and emits no Delete Forever success receipt.

Composer privacy and displayed-turn privacy are separate. Changing the composer affects only the next exchange. Retagging a displayed non-Stealth turn first authenticates its current complete owner. Tightening Standard to Private preflights and updates only exact-owned conversation rows, manifest and failure records, processed and raw representations, runtime derivatives and review records, knowledge rows, and its managed Daily Note projection; missing, edited, conflicting, or ambiguous state is untouched and returned as incomplete, and the canonical envelope changes only after that propagation succeeds. Relaxing Private to Standard changes the envelope first so any stale copy remains over-protective, then reports `reconciliation_required` if exact owned copies cannot all follow. Stealth cannot be retagged.

**Current-output export.** The browser supplies no title or content, only the exact owner tuple attached to the displayed complete exchange. The server resolves effective membership, rereads the canonical local owner while its process-local lifecycle lock is held, and matches owner, turn, chunk, privacy, and content before deriving a title from the canonical Findings text. Private Markdown remains Private-tagged and visibly labelled; Private Word/PDF content is visibly labelled. Explicit Stealth output contains neither a privacy marker nor a source identity.

**Full-Dialogue export.** The exporter discovers every canonical owner in effective history, acquires both lifecycle-lock layers for all owners in deterministic identity order, discovers again, and refuses a changed owner set or deleted owner. Every displayed Inquiry/Findings pair must match its canonical owner, local turn, chunk, privacy, and content. A Standard or Private target rejects any Stealth-owned exchange. A Stealth target may include canonically eligible inherited Standard or Private ancestry along with its own Stealth turns; the published artifact omits source and per-turn privacy markers and falls back to a neutral source-free name rather than leaking an unsafe title.

**Failure behavior.** An unresolved endpoint budget fails small. Ordinary history/contributors silently omit missing, conflicting, or ineligible pair privacy; Library/search rows may likewise be withheld without a visible notice. Ora-managed runtime derivatives with partial, malformed, conflicting, or ineligible owner claims fail before content or distance crosses lanes. OpenRouter embedding responses fail before any vector reaches a caller or Chroma unless the response is a complete one-to-one indexed set for the submitted batch: every row must be an object with a unique integer index in range, and shuffled rows are restored to input order. Every vector must be a list of finite numeric, non-Boolean components at the configured dimension; missing, duplicate, malformed, out-of-range, non-finite, or dimension-mismatched data aborts the batch.

Extraction logs authentication failure. Retag returns incomplete propagation/reconciliation; current/full export and runtime-owned review refuse invalid owners; Stealth purge reports layer failures. Normal turn/save, current-output export, and extraction coordinate through process-local Dialogue locks; envelope writes additionally take the per-conversation write lock and shared cross-process file lock. Retag, runtime-owned review, full export, and purge also take the shared `runtime_paths` cross-process lock. Optional retrieval may degrade without inventing availability. Phase 3 still neither activates Phase 4 global semantic recall nor repairs protected rows during reads. The protected-copy CLI is a separately invoked preparation path; until a separately authorized live operation builds and validates the target and changes the mapping through an appropriate cutover, the live index remains unchanged.

### Evidence & Verification

Verify this subsystem through behavior, not construction totals:

- A new fork has an empty local message list and local turn 1; effective history ends at every recorded ancestry cutoff even after ancestors receive later turns.
- Every consumer class receives the same server-authoritative history contract.
- Mixed Standard/Private turns remain distinct, and missing or conflicting pair privacy is never filled from the Dialogue tag.
- Contributor order/deduplication, fair scheduling, full-ancestry privacy, archive exclusion, missing/withheld/deferred inventory, and source-wide global exclusions hold together.
- Library, lexical, and vector candidates are authority-scoped before content or distance is exposed, including exact-owner knowledge derivatives.
- Packing respects the endpoint-safe budget, retains complete units, reuses unused 50/40/10 planning space, and fails small for an unknown endpoint.
- A retrieved conversation row returns Context + User + Assistant while its supplied vector is derived from bounded Context + User.
- Supplemental promotion changes selection only among already-validated deferred units and terminates without a numeric retry cap.
- Current-turn extraction/review derivatives preserve one owner tuple; retag reports incomplete propagation/reconciliation and exports refuse mismatch.
- Close/restore, Exit Stealth, protected purge, descendant detachment, and exact-only legacy prefix stripping produce their stated durable state.
- A plugin or video operating question retrieves the current user guide from the five-file help corpus; an unrelated query returns no help excerpt. The same source allowlist applies to indexed and lexical retrieval, and an Aside request persists neither prompt nor answer.

Ordinary `context_coverage` output is deliberately numeric: `budget`, `lanes`, `source_counts`, and optional `physical_calls`, `deferred_unit_count`, and `deduplicated_unit_count`. It has no promotion or terminal-status field and reveals no unit/source identities. The sensitive supplemental forensic trace separately retains bounded gap, query-term, and why-it-matters text plus outcome fields; private mechanics retain candidate identities for resolution and diagnosis.

The historical collection and the presence of the protected-copy CLI are not evidence of migration completion. Before declaring the stored-document/vector split operationally materialized, replay existing rows through the live contract and validate identity, counts, content shape, privacy, exclusions, and query behavior against an isolated target. Before declaring the privacy repair live, execute the authority-bound inactive copy under separate mutation authority, independently verify it, and perform a separately designed and authorized cutover.

### Expert Reading Notes

Read the boundaries in this order:

1. **Complete pair before Dialogue defaults.** The envelope owns history and pair privacy; composer/Dialogue tags cannot fill gaps. Full owner authentication belongs to fork creation, current-pair/review derivatives, runtime indexes, retag, and current/full export. Chunks/index rows are rebuildable.
2. **Eligibility before content or relevance.** Exact privacy, complete ancestry, archive state, and source-wide exclusions determine whether a unit may be read or compete. Similarity, lexical match, provenance, false metadata, and reranking cannot restore an ineligible unit.
3. **Endpoint budget before allocation.** The 200,000-token maximum is narrowed by the actual endpoint contract. Planning shares are soft and units stay whole.
4. **Stored document before vector shorthand.** Assistant content is present in the stored document even though the vector orientation excludes it.
5. **Raw commitment before interpretation.** Never manufacture an accepted-decision memory. Attribute a decision only when the user actually committed in the authoritative exchange.
6. **Help corpus before general RAG.** Aside help is a separate five-file public shelf. Do not broaden its allowlist to the vault, Dialogue collection, or private documentation merely to improve recall.

The highest drift risks are copied-parent forks, Dialogue-wide privacy fallback, a nominal cap that ignores provider reserves, contributor-count limits, row-only instead of source-wide exclusion, privacy checks after content or distance retrieval, partial derivative ownership, stale review/retag races, browser-authored export content, assistant-omitted stored documents, supplemental fresh queries, trace identity disclosure, and language that confuses Close or Exit Stealth with Delete Forever.

### Platform compatibility

The installed path is exercised on macOS/Apple Silicon. The context contract itself is provider-neutral, but these supporting surfaces retain platform dependencies:

- Local Ollama embeddings and a local reranker require their daemons and models; cloud endpoints require network access and provider credentials.
- `config/chromadb.json` binds embedder, vector dimension, and physical collection names. Every machine must keep those three consistent; dimension mismatch must fail visibly.
- ChromaDB state and the default session/conversation/vault paths are relocatable through runtime paths but remain unverified as a complete Windows-native, WSL, or Linux deployment.
- Atomic replacement is portable on one filesystem. Normal turn, current-output export, and extraction locks are process-local. Retag, runtime-owned review, full export, and Stealth purge add `runtime_paths` cross-process advisory locks; POSIX is exercised on macOS, Windows untested.
- Ora-managed Stealth purge cannot erase explicit exports or copies held by a model provider, Git remote, backup system, or other external service.
- Help indexing uses the configured embedding and Chroma services when available, but deterministic lexical fallback is local and platform-neutral. The files themselves are the canonical corpus; an unavailable index must not disable Aside.

## 9. Tool Dispatch, the Capability Gate & Execution Telemetry

- **Current gate-order and file-integrity boundary.** The dispatcher completes deterministic handler-contract and target preparation before the generic gate can consume one-shot authority.
- File read/write/edit calls require typed fields and one canonical allowed regular-or-absent target as appropriate. A write's existing ancestors must be directories; missing parent directories may still be created. That state is checked before the gate and immediately before the handler. Edits and overwrites are irreversible; only new-file creation may remain reversible.
- File write/edit use `runtime_paths.atomic_write_text` and retain an existing file's mode. A failed atomic swap leaves the old bytes intact. Overwrites advertise no rollback, and blocked/error write/edit results produce failed receipts and telemetry with `mutated=false`. Successful read text with an error-like prefix remains eligible source evidence.
- Credential calls require a declared `ora` account and an action of `status`, `store`, or `delete`; store additionally requires a non-empty string value. Invalid calls fail before gate entry.
- A blocked call reserves its dedup key only while the existing Paused card and pending authority are being written and bound. A concurrent identical call reports publication in progress, not a delivered card. Publication promotes the key to the published process-local dedup set; every failure releases the reservation, allowing an exact retry.

> **2026-08-20 G1.22 currency note.** The chapter's older composite pin remains useful for the surrounding Execution Review design, but this note supersedes its descriptions of command execution, native network reach, and MCP startup. `bash_execute` now accepts one command only, prepares one immutable argument vector, rejects shell grammar, resolves the executable against the same reduced environment used at execution, classifies Git/download/path effects semantically, and carries that prepared object through approval to `shell=False` execution. Public web and provider calls validate public destinations and redirect hops; credentialed provider requests are exact-origin and no-redirect, Jina refuses credential-bearing, signed, or sensitive-query URLs and records whether forwarding occurred, and Replicate output URLs are materialized instead of being retained as durable signed references. The vault-filesystem, Playwright, and GitHub MCP capabilities are all preserved: exact repository-local packages and Chromium are pinned, each child receives a fresh minimal environment, discovery exposes only the intersection of the child's catalog and the exact per-tool policy map, JSON-RPC requests are size-bounded and response-ID-correlated, and shutdown cleans up the children. Arbitrary Playwright code/evaluation tools are denied. The Playwright route hook is defense in depth rather than complete egress confinement: redirect hops are not delivered to the hook by the pinned browser, Chrome performs DNS independently after validation, and WebRTC and WebTransport are outside the hook.

### Problem

An orchestrator that lets a model choose what to do is only as safe as the boundary between its decisions and the world. Ora's pipeline reaches reality through many seams: it calls external models, reads RAG corpora, writes files, launches MCP subprocesses, invokes paid image and video APIs, runs user-installed shell hooks, and fetches the open web. Without a common enforcement-and-audit layer, six failure modes are unguarded, and each is independently sufficient to cause harm.

First, **auto-approve carrying a dangerous action.** The dispatcher has a permission mode (approve-each, auto-approve). If enforcement lives inside the permission mode, then flipping to auto-approve — a convenience toggle — silently authorizes irreversible deletes, secret reads, and unknown actions. A permission mode is a UX affordance; it must not be the security boundary.

Second, **unaudited execution.** A tool that runs and leaves no record cannot be reviewed after the fact. When a session does something surprising, "what did it actually touch, in what order, with what result" must be reconstructable from a durable log, not from the model's own narration of what it believes it did. Model narration is not evidence; it is a claim by the same process under review.

Third, **secret leakage into logs.** The instrument built to prevent the first two problems becomes the third if it records a retrieved API key, a password in a command argument, or a token embedded in a git URL. An audit log that captures secrets is a secret store with worse access control.

Fourth, **silent failures dissolving into empty strings.** A RAG read that returns nothing, a Step-1 parse that fails, a capability slot every provider refuses — each can degrade to `""` and flow downstream as if the stage simply had nothing to say. The user sees a thinner answer and never learns a subsystem broke.

Fifth, **non-reproducible tool selection during evaluation.** Comparative evaluation runs the same prompt through different model configurations. If tool selection is itself a model decision, the tools fired vary per run and per model, and the comparison is contaminated — you are no longer measuring the model, you are measuring the model plus its improvised tool use.

Sixth, **ungoverned web reach.** Reaching the open web from inside an analysis raises three distinct risks: leaking the prompt to a search provider, double-charging paid fetch/image APIs on retries, and injecting unattributed web text into a grounded answer with no provenance.

This chapter's subsystem — the dispatcher seam, the capability gate, the tool-event recorder, the capability-slot registry, the MCP client, the three web-reach modules, and the per-turn trace/health surfaces — exists to close all six. Its core (`orchestrator/tool_events.py`) is Phase 1 of the Execution Review architecture: the always-on instrumentation-and-enforcement substrate.

### Design Decision & Tradeoffs

**One always-on module for manifest, recorder, and gate.** The capability manifest (the risk vocabulary), the tool-event recorder (the audit write), and the pre-execution gate (the enforcement decision) all live in `tool_events.py`, deliberately in one module because they share one lifecycle (`tool_events.py:1-34`). The alternative — three modules, wired independently — was rejected because the three must never drift apart: the gate decides using the same axes the recorder stamps, and a recorder that logged a different classification than the gate enforced would make the audit trail lie about what was enforced. Colocation is the mechanism that keeps the enforced classification and the recorded classification identical by construction.

**Three risk axes layered on the dispatcher's function axis, not replacing it.** The dispatcher already classifies every tool as read/write/execute — the *function* axis. This subsystem adds three orthogonal axes on top and leaves the function axis untouched (`tool_events.py:8-15`): *mutability* (`read` → `reversible_write` → `external_write` → `irreversible`), *sensitivity* (`public` → `private` → `sensitive` → `secret`), and *egress* (`none` → `local` → `external`). The reason for orthogonality: "can I undo it," "how secret is what it touches," and "does data leave the machine" are independent questions, and collapsing them into one severity scalar loses the distinction that lets the gate block an irreversible-but-public action and an innocuous-but-secret action for different, legible reasons. The rejected alternative — a single risk score — cannot express "reversible write to a secret file," which must be gated on sensitivity while a reversible write to a public file passes.

**The gate runs before execution and independently of the permission mode.** This is the release-blocking decision. `gate()` is called at the dispatcher seam *before* the tool runs, and it does not consult the dispatcher's approve-each/auto-approve setting at all (`tool_events.py:836-850`). Auto-approve can never carry an irreversible, unknown, or secret action, because the gate that blocks those never sees the permission mode. The permission mode governs the *routine* read/reversible tools; the gate governs the *dangerous* ones, and the two are separate code paths on purpose.

**Fail closed on the unknown.** An action absent from the manifest is classified `irreversible` + `secret` + `external` + `unknown: True` and gated (`tool_events.py:99-106`, `manifest_axes` at 194-199). The tradeoff is explicit and accepted: a newly added tool whose axes nobody declared will be blocked until someone classifies it. That is the safe failure — a false block costs a human approval; a false allow costs whatever the unclassified action did. This is the Unix `umask`-style default: deny first, grant deliberately.

**Deterministic tool selection, with a default-off model-driven escape hatch.** Tool selection splits into two lanes. The deterministic lane (`tool_selector.py`) derives parameters mechanically from the prompt — a mode declares tool needs in a `## TOOLS` section, and `web_fetch` fires on exactly the URLs present in the prompt (`tool_selector.py:70-71`, docstring at 26). This is reproducible: the same prompt fires the same tools regardless of which model runs, which is what makes it safe during comparative evaluation. The model-driven escape hatch (a model requesting read-category tools mid-analysis) exists but is gated behind `ORA_MODEL_TOOL_SELECTION`, which defaults to `"0"` (`boot.py:8376`). It is built and wired, not active. That makes it **partial**, not implemented — a flag that defaults off is a capability the installed system does not exercise.

**Pattern B web evidence over Pattern A tool-use round-trips.** All three web-reach modules inject retrieved evidence into the system prompt as a context block rather than exposing web search as a tool the model calls in a round-trip (`claim_verification.py:14-22`). Pattern B was chosen over Pattern A for provider-agnosticism (a local model with no tool-use protocol still gets the evidence), traceability (every injected chunk carries a provenance tier), and cost control (the fetch happens once, deterministically, not once per model retry). The tradeoff: the model cannot adaptively decide it needs one more search mid-generation. For Ora's grounded-answer goal, deterministic-evidence-then-generate beats adaptive-search-during-generate, because the former is auditable and the latter is not.

**Capability slots generalize the hard-coded vision-extraction routing.** Rather than hard-code each image/video provider into the dispatcher, `capability_registry.py` lifts the existing `vision_extraction` routing pattern into a named-slot registry: `capabilities.json` declares N slots with input/output contracts, and `routing-config.json` declares a preferred→fallback provider chain per slot (`capability_registry.py:13-21`). `invoke()` contract-validates inputs before any provider runs and walks the fallback chain on fallback-triggering error codes. The alternative — provider conditionals scattered through the dispatcher — was rejected because it made adding a provider a dispatcher edit and gave no uniform place to record the invocation or express a fallback chain.

**The tradeoff of the whole design:** fail-closed enforcement plus a single instrumentation seam adds friction and concentrates correctness risk. Every blocked action needs a human approval. Every action pays a record cost. And the entire enforcement boundary lives in one module — a bug in `gate()` is a bug in the security boundary. I accept this concentration deliberately: one auditable seam I can test exhaustively beats enforcement scattered across every call site, where coverage is impossible to reason about.

**Open problems.** Four are real and unresolved. First, the action manifest, the exact MCP per-tool policy maps, and the protected-path lists must still be kept in sync with new actions and protection-worthy files; an unrecognized MCP tool is not exposed and an unclassified action fails closed, but a newly protection-worthy file omitted from `_PROTECTED_FILES` remains ungated. Second, the secret-path patterns are POSIX-dotfile-shaped and will under-classify Windows credential conventions (`%APPDATA%` stores) at the gate — a correctness gap the moment anyone ports off Mac. Third, the evidence-runner that would let the gate verify a check declaration before allowing it is declared vocabulary only — the runner itself is Phase 5 and not built. Fourth, the Playwright route hook cannot see redirect hops, cannot bind Chrome's subsequent DNS resolution to Python's validation result, and does not cover WebRTC or WebTransport; complete browser egress confinement would require a separately approved proxy, firewall/network sandbox, or maintained browser interception fork.

### Implementation

**The dispatcher seam.** `dispatcher.py` is the single wiring point where axes are derived and the gate and recorder fire. For an MCP call, `tool_events.mcp_policy` resolves the exact namespaced tool plus its actual parameters into call-specific axes and authority scopes; an absent or malformed policy fails closed rather than borrowing a server-wide default. File targets still escalate through `resolve_path_sensitivity`, and protected configuration still escalates mutability. For `bash_execute`, the seam prepares the command once and binds that exact prepared object — argument vector, working directory, reduced-environment digest, executable identity, semantic selectors, and read/write scopes — to both the gate and the later execution. It calls `tool_events.gate(...)` before any effect and records the real result after execution. The credential-store channel remains the one sanctioned secret path: a matching standing allow can bypass the per-call prompt while still being logged existence-only. The gate runs *here*, before the tool, so no downstream permission mode can reach around it.

**The gate decision.** `gate()` (`tool_events.py:836-933`) computes a `block_why`: unknown → blocked; `irreversible` → blocked; `secret` → blocked; and, for model-facing surfaces only, `sensitive` → blocked (851-863). If nothing blocks, it returns `allowed` immediately. If something blocks, it tries three escalations in order: consume a matching one-shot approval token if one was previously granted (884-889); ask a live human via an `interactive_approver` callback when one is wired — the terminal approve-each prompt or a browser callback (893-912); otherwise deny immediately and write a Paused queue entry (916-933). The third path is a *record, never a wait* — the gate does not block the thread waiting for approval; it denies now and leaves an entry the user can approve later, at which point a re-issued matching call passes once. A per-process dedup set (`_queued_hashes`, 788-803) ensures an agentic retry loop enqueues one Paused card, not one per retry.

**Approval tokens.** A grant mints a one-shot token keyed by `(action, args_hash)` with a default 3600-second TTL (`tool_events.py:574`, `grant_approval` 610-634). Consumption marks it used and stamps `used_at` (`check_and_consume_approval` 738-771). Conversation-scoped tokens require an exact conversation match — a context-less call cannot consume a token minted for a specific conversation (751-757). The read-modify-write is wrapped in a cross-platform exclusive lock (`_with_approvals_lock` → `runtime_paths.locked_file`) plus atomic `os.replace`; the lock is retained rather than replaced by atomic-write alone because grant and consume both mutate the same file and atomic replace does not prevent a lost update between concurrent grant/consume (601-607, `_save_approvals` 593-598). The standing-allow variant (637-657) is the credential-store exception: revocable, scoped per service, logged existence-only.

**Risk tier (Execution Review Phase 2).** The per-turn risk tier rides in the turn context (`set_turn_context(..., risk_tier=...)`, `tool_events.py:375-390`) so the gate and every recorded event see it without a dispatcher signature change. Because the tier is known only after mode dispatch — which runs after the context is seeded at step 2 — `update_turn_risk_tier` (393-405) attaches it in place. `record()` stamps it onto every event (528-532), and `consume_token_by_fingerprint` (681-712) implements the Phase-2 task gate, consuming a token by `(action, args_hash)` alone (the fingerprint itself binds the conversation, so the mint and the resume may legitimately differ). This is verified by `test_risk_gate.py` (93 tests) and `test_risk_gate_pipeline.py` (6).

**Turn context propagation.** The context (`trace_dir`, `conversation_id`, `stealth`, `surface`, `risk_tier`) is stored in a `ContextVar` (`tool_events.py:371`). The pipeline seeds it at step 2 and the server at the `_direct_stream` head. It propagates to Gear-4 worker threads via `contextvars.copy_context` (through `boot._submit_with_context`, `boot.py:44`) and to child project-tool processes via `ORA_*` environment variables read back by `get_turn_context` when no context var is set (408-416). This is why a tool-event fired deep inside a parallel Gear-4 stream still knows which conversation it belongs to.

**Two redaction layers.** Sensitivity-based redaction (`_redact_for_record`, `tool_events.py:463-490`): a `secret` event is reduced to existence-only (`content_redacted: "secret-existence-only"`, args and reads dropped); a `sensitive` event keeps only length descriptors. Content-based scrub (`scrub_content`, 359-366) runs regardless of sensitivity, matching OpenAI/Anthropic-style keys, GitHub PATs, AWS access-key ids, Slack tokens, bearer headers, userinfo credentials in URLs, and `password=`/`token=` assignments (`_SCRUB_PATTERNS` 348-356). The path classifier (`resolve_path_sensitivity`, 324-340) matches boundary-anchored patterns — `\.env($|\.)` catches `.env` and `prod.env` but not `.venv`; patterns are anchored so `tokenizer.json` does not match "token" (289-305). Paths under known private roots (workspace, vault, conversations, `~/Documents`, `~/sites`, scratch) classify `private`; unrecognized territory classifies `sensitive` — conservative by default (317-340).

**Telemetry health.** `record()` never raises (`tool_events.py:508-569`): on failure it calls `_note_failure`, which increments a counter, records the last error, prints to stderr, and best-effort emits a `ToolEventRecordingFailure` on the oversight bus (426-439). `get_telemetry_health()` (442-446) reports `incomplete: True` once any failure has occurred, and every subsequent event is stamped `telemetry_incomplete: True` (544-546). The failure mode this prevents: a silently-dropped audit line that makes the log look complete when it is not. When telemetry breaks, the log says so, on every line thereafter. `ORA_TOOL_EVENTS=off` disables the *write* only (516) — the gate still runs and still fails closed. The dual sink routes to the active turn trace dir when a turn is live, else `data/tool-events.jsonl` (`_sink_path` 502-505). Writes use `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_APPEND|O_CREAT, 0o644)` with UTF-8-encoded lines capped at `MAX_LINE_BYTES` (8000), truncating to a keep-set of structural fields when over (549-567).

**Capability-slot registry.** `load_registry` (`capability_registry.py:744`) reads `capabilities.json` and `routing-config.json`'s `slots` block (runtime-overlay-aware), merges project-declared slots (`merge_project_capability_slots` 674), resolves slot inheritance (`resolve_slot_inheritance` 565), and auto-registers `local_diffusers` best-effort (879-880). Six integration modules bind real handlers via `register()` — `gemini_images`, `openai_images`, `openrouter_images`, `stability`, `replicate`, and `local_diffusers`. `invoke()` (361-524) validates required inputs before any handler runs, fills optional defaults, builds the provider chain (preferred→fallback, or a length-1 chain when an explicit `provider_id` disables fallback), and walks it: on a `CapabilityError` whose code is in the slot's fallback-triggering set and with providers remaining, it advances; otherwise it breaks and re-raises the last error with the full `attempts` chain attached, so the caller can render "Slot 1 refused for content_policy → Slot 2 unavailable → no providers left." Every invocation records one `capability_slot` tool-event with the provider tried, the attempts list, and the exit reason (`_record_tool_event` 527-558). `config/capabilities.json` defines 14 slots; the image and video families are wired via integrations, while project-specific slots (e.g. `column_authoring_paid`, `image_generates_barb_cartoon`) have their provider wiring outside `~/ora` in the project's `ora-project/`, so their runtime provider status is per-install.

**MCP client.** `mcp_client.py` accepts only the three pinned stdio declarations in `config/mcp-servers.json`: vault filesystem (`@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem` 2026.7.10), Playwright (`@playwright/mcp` 0.0.79), and GitHub (`@modelcontextprotocol/server-github` 2025.4.8). Startup resolves their exact Node entrypoints under `mcp-runtime/node_modules`; it never invokes `npm`, `npx`, or a runtime package download. The desktop and server installers apply `mcp-runtime/patch-playwright.cjs` after installing the pinned packages, then provision the locked Playwright Chromium through the repository-local Playwright CLI. Startup refuses an unpatched connector. Each child starts in its designated working directory with a fresh allowlisted environment; the GitHub token is inherited only by the GitHub child, and a missing token skips that child without removing the other two capabilities. Discovery namespaces tools as `mcp_<server>_<tool>` and exposes only tools present in both the child catalog and the exact policy map. Requests are serialized and bounded, replies must carry the exact outstanding JSON-RPC ID and shape, discovery/schema/catalog and stderr retention have explicit limits, tool errors remain failures, and shutdown closes streams, terminates or kills stragglers within bounded waits, joins reader threads, and clears the catalog. A server that cannot start fails soft and does not block Ora startup. HTTP transport is unsupported; pinned stdio is the only accepted transport.

For GitHub repository creation and forks without an explicit organization, the dispatcher prepares the actual child before review and binds its non-secret launch identity in the existing protected request, without changing the raw argument hash. The approved call uses that exact child and its prepared arguments; replacement makes the approval stale, and execution never silently recovers onto another child after approval. A child lost after the start receipt produces a failed effect. This preserves the implicit account destination across review and execution without recording a credential fingerprint or claiming to verify a GitHub login. Explicit repository/organization arguments do not require remote-content snapshots. Browser target preflight rejects missing, non-string, or empty required element targets, incomplete drag targets, and malformed form-field targets before the gate; optional no-target tools, empty form lists, and upload cancellation remain supported. Supported Page-relative typed interactions additionally bind the exact child, Page, document, and native element or intrinsic target before approval. The pinned connector retains those targets across native waits, retries, and dialog handoffs and reauthenticates them through its final native send, including keyboard focus, file chooser, and history targets. At the final native guard, native-fill insertion or deletion requires the complete bound text target to remain selected; supported email and number controls, whose selection Chromium hides, are reselected on that exact bound, focused control there. Slow typing keeps the narrower focus/containment rule. A modified click retains both the original control-rooted focus outcome and a separately bound retargeted button/link receiver for trailing modifier keyup; unrelated focus remains rejected. It preserves native interaction behavior without switching to another Page or replacement document; arbitrary browser code remains denied. Chromium can still reload after the final connector check and before applying an already-sent action, so browser-atomic execution is not claimed.

**Web reach at steps 2/5/8.** `web_consultation.py` (F-Consult) runs at Step 2: parallel intent searches, source tiering (whitelisted/corroborated/single/excluded), per-chunk provenance, optional prompt-sanity and conflict passes; dependency-injected, enabled by default via `routing-config` (`web_consultation.enabled: true`; wired at `boot.py:7459`). `web_extraction.py` escalates when high-trust snippets are too thin: it deep-fetches full pages, chunks, fit-gates, and folds a `### DEEP EXTRACTIONS` block into web context — fail-*closed* when nothing survives the fit gate (`web_extraction.py:382-388`). It is gated on `ORA_WEB_EXTRACTION` — code default off (`boot.py:7042`, `_env_flag`) but force-exported `=1` by the tracked launcher `start.sh:26`, so it is **active on a normally-launched installed system**. `claim_verification.py` runs a pre-flight for Step 5 (Reviser) and Step 8 (Verifier): it parses the evaluator's `## FLAGGED CLAIMS`, runs challenge queries in parallel via `web_search_structured` through the configured provider cascade, and injects `## FLAGGED CLAIM EVIDENCE` into the reviser's and verifier's prompts — Pattern B (`claim_verification.py:1-22`, wired at `boot.py:11278/12008/12012`). The shared `network_policy` now normalizes destinations, refuses credentials in URLs and non-public address resolutions, and requires explicit validation of each allowed redirect hop. Authenticated OpenRouter and Replicate calls are bound to their exact provider origin and do not forward credentials across redirects; public provider-returned assets are fetched without the provider bearer. Jina remains available for useful public pages, but refuses credential-bearing, signed, or sensitive-query source URLs and emits durable forwarding/refusal metadata. Replicate's asynchronous output walker materializes URL values and URL-shaped dictionary keys into local artifacts before job state is retained.

**Search provider cascade and its two environment overrides.** Which provider actually answers a search is decided by `search_cascade_order` in `routing-config.json`, walked in order by `_search_text` (`orchestrator/tools/web_search.py`). A provider whose API key is absent is skipped; a provider that errors *or* returns an empty result falls through to the next tier, so a free primary can sit in front of a paid fallback that only answers what the primary misses. Two environment variables override the configured behaviour per deployment, because `ORA_ROUTING_CONFIG_PATH` replaces the whole routing-config file rather than merging into it, and forking a 500KB config to change one list invites silent drift:

- `ORA_SEARCH_CASCADE_ORDER` — comma-separated provider names (for example `ddg,brave`) replacing `search_cascade_order` for that process. Matching is case-insensitive. Names that match no registered provider are dropped with a stderr warning naming them, and the remaining names are used; only when nothing at all is recognised is the whole override discarded and the routing-config value used instead. Note what that fallback is worth: the shipped `routing-config.json` holds the same list as the built-in default, so falling back does not avoid a paid-first cascade — it restores one. That is why the resolved order is now logged on every path, whichever source supplied it.
- `ORA_SEARCH_SEMANTIC_AUGMENT` — `0`/`1`, overriding the `semantic_augment` block. This exists because semantic augmentation is a **second, independent search per query** — `_gather_raw` calls `_search_text` again pinned to the semantic provider — and `search_cascade_order` does not govern it. Without this override, pinning a free keyword cascade for cost reasons would still bill one semantic call per query, with nothing in the cascade setting to indicate it.
- `ORA_SEARCH_MAX_QUERIES` — the maximum number of search queries a single fan-out may issue. `0`, unset, or malformed means unlimited, so a machine that never sets it keeps its existing behaviour. The ceiling matters because fan-out width is chosen by a model — the intent list at Step 2, the flagged-claim list at Step 4.5 — and neither has a natural bound; production has recorded a single fan-out issuing 213 searches. Enforcement is at the fan-out sites, which know what they are dropping and record it in their trace signals, and it fails **open**: excess queries are dropped and the run continues, since both callers already treat missing web evidence as a coverage gap rather than an error.

- `ORA_SEARCH_MAX_QUERIES_<STAGE>` — a per-stage ceiling that overrides the global one, currently honoured for `UNFLAGGED`. Not every fan-out is worth the same: verifying the claims an evaluator actually flagged is the check the pipeline has always run, while the Step-5.5 unflagged scan is a speculative second pass that hunts for more and fans out twice per gear-4 run on top of it. They cost the same per query and are not worth the same, so `ORA_SEARCH_MAX_QUERIES_UNFLAGGED=3` can bind the speculative pass while flagged claims keep the global 12. Unset or malformed defers to `ORA_SEARCH_MAX_QUERIES`, so a typo cannot silently uncap the more expensive stage; an explicit `0` means that stage is deliberately unlimited.

- `ORA_SEARCH_DDG_CONCURRENCY` — how many DuckDuckGo requests may be in flight at once; default 3, and `0` restores unbounded behaviour. DuckDuckGo is reached by scraping its HTML endpoint and throttles on **burst** concurrency far more than on steady volume, while the pipeline's fan-outs size their thread pool to the number of items — so a single claim-verification pass could open a dozen simultaneous connections. Production recorded 121 `operation timed out` responses plus connection resets in two hours that way, and because a failed tier cascades, each of those became a request to the next, billed provider. Pacing converts those timeouts back into results, which both restores coverage and removes the spend they were causing.

The cascade order is read once per process and cached, so changing it takes effect at restart rather than on edit. The cap is read from the environment on every call and is not cached, but since a process's environment does not change under it, that distinction only matters to tests. Provider selection is logged when a later tier answers and when a configured tier is inactive for want of a key — previously both were silent, which meant a cascade could quietly degrade, or quietly bill, with nothing in the logs to say which provider had served a given query.

**Forensic trace and health banner.** `pipeline_trace.py` writes per-turn step JSON/MD, RAG-failure logs, and a step-health summary under `data/pipeline-traces/<conv>/<ts>/`, gated by `ORA_PIPELINE_TRACE` (default on, `pipeline_trace.py:58-66`). `_resolve_ora_home` derives the root from `__file__` rather than a literal `~`, avoiding the observed bug where an unset `HOME` produced a literal-tilde directory (35-56). Stealth conversations create no trace directory at all (`start_trace`, 75-101). `pipeline_health.py` is the silent-failure surface: any pipeline step calls `record(category, message)` into thread-local storage (`_tls`, 32-47), and `collect_and_clear` drains it at turn end to a chat banner (50-63). This is what turns "empty RAG dissolved into an empty string" into a warning the user actually sees.

**Lifecycle hooks.** `hooks.py` loads definitions from the effective `runtime_paths.CONFIG_DIR/hooks` directory. Its `fire_hooks` function runs their shell scripts for pre_tool/post_tool/session_*/pre_compact via `subprocess.run(command, shell=True, ...)`, using `runtime_paths.WORKSPACE` as the working directory and recording each execution as a tool-event with `enforcement: boundary_only`. The shipped `session-cleanup.json` imports `bash_execute.cleanup_all` from `orchestrator/tools` relative to that working directory, so a relocated installation uses its own hook configuration and cleanup module even when a separate `~/ora` exists. Only `pre_tool`, `post_tool`, and `pre_compact` may inject output. A `pre_tool` injection reaches the next model receiver exactly once while structured tool results and deterministic consumers stay valid; a `pre_compact` injection reaches the first system context exactly once (creating one if absent) and persists through success and every fail-soft return. Because `session_start` and `session_end` have no receiver, definitions requesting `inject_output` for either are refused before execution; ordinary non-injecting session hooks continue. Ora cannot see inside these `shell=True` subprocesses, so their axes are honestly marked boundary-only. User-installed hooks are pre-authorized, while installing hook JSON remains a protected-config write (`config/hooks` is in `_PROTECTED_PREFIXES`, `tool_events.py:256`), closing the "one allowed write buys arbitrary execution" escalation.

**URL import.** `url_import.py` wraps `yt-dlp` to download public media into a per-conversation library. `YTDLP_BINARY = shutil.which("yt-dlp") or "/opt/homebrew/bin/yt-dlp"` (48) — `which` finds a binary on any platform, but the fallback and the not-found guidance (`brew install yt-dlp`, 149) are Homebrew-specific and Mac-shaped.

**Config surfaces.** `config/capabilities.json` (slot contracts; protected), `config/mcp-servers.json` (the three pinned stdio declarations plus exact per-tool policies; protected), `mcp-runtime/package-lock.json` (exact MCP dependency graph), and `config/routing-config.json` (`web_consultation.enabled`, slot chains; protected). Env flags: `ORA_TOOL_EVENTS`, `ORA_PIPELINE_TRACE`, `ORA_MODEL_TOOL_SELECTION`, `ORA_WEB_EXTRACTION*`, `ORA_RISK_TIER`.

### Evidence & Verification

The 2026-08-20 G1.22 pre-channel acceptance used focused behavioral checks rather than repinning the whole chapter. The command/native-network slice passed 126 tests with 3 skips plus 186 parameterized subtests, and its execution-provenance suite passed 39 tests. The final MCP correction passed 91 tests plus 64 parameterized subtests; the wider preceding MCP run passed 251 tests plus 41 parameterized subtests. An independent MCP review returned accepted after checking that all three capabilities remained available, policy was per tool and call, arbitrary Playwright code was denied, errors stayed failures, and no child survived cleanup. These counts establish only the pre-channel boundary. The original Order-16 hardening is merge `88f971ae6067289fbfadd6f681a481481d063919`; the focused ordinary-use follow-up is separate and landed at Ora merge `0c602b439fb62b1892611cd1ec4c41a4b9587ed1` (PR #410). Full G1.22 remains yellow and open for live channel, browser-transport, and supported-platform proof. No reactive memory watchdog, CPU/per-file/scratch quota, or extra HTTP/Jina/global MCP cap is present or being added absent ordinary-use evidence.

The subsystem carries roughly 400 unit tests across the target files, confirmed by `grep -c 'def test'` at 7a5e8f40: `test_tool_events` (52), `test_dispatcher_gate` (44), `test_capability_registry` (34) + `test_capability_registry_fallback` (25), `test_web_consultation` (44), `test_web_extraction` (21), `test_claim_verification` (35), `test_tool_selector` (22), `test_mcp_catalog` (15), `test_url_import` (13), `test_risk_gate` (93), `test_risk_gate_pipeline` (6) — 404 in total. (The inventory listed a `test_capability_registry_routes_rerun` file and a smaller risk-gate count; at this commit that file is absent and the risk-gate suite is larger — trust the tree.)

To confirm the gate fails closed: run `test_dispatcher_gate` and `test_risk_gate`. The gate's block logic (`tool_events.py:851-863`) and its independence from the permission mode are the properties under test; the `FAIL_CLOSED` default (99-106) is exercised by feeding an unclassified action and asserting a block.

To confirm telemetry flags its own incompleteness: force a `record()` failure and read `get_telemetry_health()` — `incomplete` flips to `True` and every subsequent event carries `telemetry_incomplete: True` (544-546). This is the check that distinguishes a complete log from a log that merely looks complete.

To confirm slot fallback records an attempts chain: `test_capability_registry_fallback` drives a Slot-1 refusal into a Slot-2 attempt and asserts the re-raised error's `.attempts` captures the full chain (`capability_registry.py:361-524`).

**Explicitly untested surfaces.** There is no dedicated test file for `pipeline_trace.py`, `pipeline_health.py`, or `hooks.py` at this commit (confirmed by directory listing) — the trace atomic-write, the stealth-skip path, and the hook telemetry-recording path are exercised only indirectly through pipeline tests, not in isolation. `ORA_MODEL_TOOL_SELECTION` is default-off and not exported by any launcher, so the installed system does not exercise it; `ORA_WEB_EXTRACTION` is code-default-off but force-exported `=1` by `start.sh:26`, so a normally-launched installed system does run web extraction. The evidence-runner has a validated check vocabulary (`validate_check_declaration`, `EVIDENCE_RUNNER_DEFAULTS`, `tool_events.py:983-1005`) but no runner — an undeclared check is un-runnable by construction, and the runner is Phase 5 (980-981). No reliability budget is instrumented anywhere in this subsystem: there is no per-step success-rate metric, no gate-block-rate counter beyond the raw event log, and no measured coverage percentage. The failure modes above are prevented qualitatively — the gate blocks the class, the recorder logs the class — and the only quantitative instrument present is the telemetry-failure counter, which counts recorder failures, not action success rates.

**Post-pin trace verification.** The dedicated tests absent at `7a5e8f40` now exist in `orchestrator/tests/test_trace_manifest.py` and `test_trace_walk_ui.py`. The PR #269 acceptance run exercised both import orders and the full suite with exact baseline failure/error signature parity.
